## 4. Why it cannot deadlock

Every wait in the process:

| # | who waits | for what | why it ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | the controller, phase 5 | a `poll` of its end eventfd and its wake eventfd for at most 1 s, then, whatever ended it, a read of the end eventfd (`EAGAIN` as 0) and the end channel's `try_recv`s until `Empty`; in `block_on`, a failure record's write on its blocking pool (2.4) | every dispatched attempt or peek sends exactly one end message (2.4), then writes the end eventfd, and attempts and peeks only send, on a channel of capacity `threads`, which the controller only drains; the write is a blocking-pool task, which ends by W5, and `block_on` selects on the controller's wake eventfd (W2) |
| W2 | the controller, in `block_on` (phases 2 to 7) | network requests, and its blocking-pool tasks: a local source's copy and a URL stage's chunk writes (2.5), a failure record's write (phases 4 and 5, 2.4), phase 6's tasks, a hashing pass, a directory transfer and a store archive's central-directory chunk writes (2.9) | a staging try ends at its idle timeout or below its rate floor, its own writes aside (W5), and its bytes are capped by the spool's size (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 1.4); a store request has the sink's `timeout_ms` (1.6); retries are counted; a blocking-pool task waits at most for a thread to finish its own task, its pool having a thread for every task it can have outstanding (2.11), so it ends by W5, or by W17 for a lookup; `block_on` selects on the controller's wake eventfd, and the controller then cancels its runtime's token itself (2.11) |
| W3 | an idle worker | the next attempt or peek on the dispatch channel | it holds nothing, and the controller dispatches before it waits whenever a slot fits |
| W4 | an attempt or a peek | a core lock | held only to look up or publish, never across a decode or another lock; the only caller code under one is the budget, which is atomics (core 4.1) |
| W5 | an attempt, a peek (its staged read, its peek file's write and `fchmod`), the controller, the finisher, a blocking-pool thread, the main thread (its read of `JOB.json`, and step 6's procfs and cgroup reads) | a local filesystem call | A11; a buffered write can be held for minutes by dirty-page throttling, which `_exit` ends for the engine's own writes, but not a wait on a file lock another, throttled process holds (2.11, *What cannot be bounded*) |
| W6 | a stage, a transfer, a hashing pass (an archive's first pass, a directory destination's hash), the start's aborts (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 6.2), a LIST, a preflight request, or one of the controller's moved writes (a failure record's file, a phase-6 task, 2.11) | a `download_concurrency` permit (not a LIST, a preflight request or a moved write), the spare permit's token (a LIST, the bucket proof's among them, preflight's head read and its `running` write, a moved write, which waits at most behind a stale lookup on the spare permit, W17), or the phase budget | held only by stages and the start's aborts, which end by W2, and by transfers, a store's inside requests and their lookups, which end by W2 and W17, and a directory's inside file calls and its commit, which end by W5 and W15, by a hashing pass, which ends by W5, or by a LIST, which ends by W2, its page then dropped by the merge before its next LIST (W19); the spare by one LIST, preflight request or moved write at a time, which ends by W2, or by W5 for a moved write; a permit whose token is out comes back, and a token goes home, when its lookup returns (W17); and no holder waits for a permit or a reservation (2.9) |
| W7 | a transfer or the finisher | the journal's mutex | held for one append or one removal, never across a request; the file call inside is W5, which throttling can hold for minutes, so the finisher takes the mutex by a polled `try_lock` that gives up at `T_hard - head_reserve` (W10). The outcomes files take no lock (2.9) |
| W8 | the progress thread, the main thread for an `exit` line it writes itself, and its writer thread (2.11) | stdout | nothing waits on the progress thread but the finisher and the main thread's failed-spawn hand-over, each for at most 1 s; without a deadline the main thread's own write follows at most 1 s of `poll` on a non-blocking description, or, when stdout cannot be reopened, goes through its writer thread, waited on for at most 1 s, and with one every early line is its writer thread's, whatever the descriptor, which it waits on for at most `min(1 s, the time left - 500 ms)`, not at all when that is not positive, before `exit_now` (2.11) |
| W9 | the lifecycle thread | the self-pipe and its `timerfd`, both non-blocking; stdin, under `stop_on_stdin_eof`, is the stdin watch's own thread's (2.11), whose read, should another reader outrun it, holds only that thread | nothing waits on it; it does no I/O but those reads, its eventfd writes and `exit_now`, whose in-place disconnects never wait for an owner, and, in a test build, its two trace lines to the lifecycle trace FIFO; it writes no regular file but a held `point`'s `reached` line, which it appends to the `trace` log before it waits, a write made only at a hold the test arms, on a thread the test already holds, so it delays only a held thread and never a `T_hard` the run relies on; its wakes never block and allocate nothing: atomic stores, the stop word's among them, and one `write` to each wake eventfd, non-blocking, whose counter two writes never fill; it sends on no channel, takes no lock and unparks no thread, so no receiver's mutex or allocation can hold it; it calls no tokio wake (2.11) |
| W10 | the finisher | its hand-over; the journal's mutex (W7); a permit for each abort, and abort requests; for each of the manifest's writes, a permit whose token is home, and the write | the hand-over comes from the controller: after phase 7, at a stop, at a 69, 7 or 74 before the `running` head, or from its panic handler; each request is on its own runtime and client; a permit's or token's wait ends by W17, and once `T_hard` is armed every wait, for a permit, a token or a try, also ends at its deadline, its wake eventfd's readiness registered before it reads `T_hard` (2.11): step 1's polled `try_lock` of the journal's mutex at `T_hard - head_reserve`, which then skips steps 1 and 2, leaving the aborts to the next start; step 2's at its cut, `T_hard - head_reserve`, each request then being one try, ending by `T_hard - head_reserve`, the head's by `T_hard - 2 s` (2.11 steps 2 and 3); file calls are W5 |
| W11 | the spool's and the output root's locks | `flock` with `LOCK_NB` | never waits: busy exits 69 |
| W12 | the controller, after its hand-over | nothing: it parks | the finisher's or the lifecycle thread's `_exit` |
| W13 | any thread | stderr | never waits but for a W5 call: the panic hook writes nothing, and the engine's writer takes no lock (2.11 step 2) and blocks on no descriptor but a regular file or a block device (2.11). Writeback can hold such a write for minutes (A11), so the lifecycle thread writes none there, the controller writes its stop's line only after its hand-over, a worker or a peek its run failure's only after its `request_stop`, the finisher its stat-error warning only after step 4's wait, before step 5, and the main thread its early lines through its writer thread when a deadline is set (2.4, 2.11, *A held stdout*). Rust's own abort message may wait on a full socket until `T_hard`, and be held on a regular file (A13) |
| W14 | a requester of a stop | the self-pipe | never waits: the pipe is non-blocking, and a full pipe already holds a stop |
| W15 | a blocking-pool thread's commit, or the controller at its hand-over | the directory sink's commit lock | held for one flag read and at most one rename and one `pwrite` (W5); the controller then keeps it, parked (W12), and a commit that waits on it after the hand-over waits, holding nothing, until `_exit` |
| W16 | the controller, the finisher or the main thread (an attempt takes no engine lock, 2.12), or the progress thread | the progress mailbox's mutex | held for one swap of the slot, never across a write to stdout |
| W17 | a runtime's blocking-pool thread, and the next try under the lookup's permit, which waits for its lookup token (2.5) | name resolution (`getaddrinfo`, which the HTTP service runs there in a task of its own, 2.5: staging's and publishing's on the controller's runtime, the finisher's on its own) | the C resolver's timeouts and attempts (A17), and `T_hard`: a request's timeout drops its future, not the lookup, which keeps its token until it returns; no other blocking task waits behind it (2.11) |
| W18 | the main thread | the controller's join | it never returns; on a return the main thread requests a stop `internal` and parks; `_exit` ends it (2.11) |
| W19 | resume's scheduler | the merge's position (2.9) | the merge waits only for its LISTs, by W6, W2 and W17, never on the scheduler or a hashing pass: a LIST's lookup has its runtime's spare permit (2.11) |
| W20 | the main thread at step 7, and each of both pools' start tasks (2.11) | the start count reaching `2 x download_concurrency + 2` | a timed wait that ends 1 s after the first spawn whatever the count; a shortfall then exits 69 (`thread_spawn_failed`) |

**There is no cycle.** Rank the parties: 0, the kernel, the network and stdout; 1, core locks, the budgets and the engine's mutexes; 2, a running attempt or peek; 3, transfers, the blocking-pool tasks and the finisher; 4, the controller, its stages, the start's aborts, the LISTs and preflight's requests; 5, the main thread, whose waits are W5's, W8's, W16's and W18's join on the controller; and the lifecycle thread apart. Every wait-for edge goes to a lower rank, or to rank 0 with a timeout, except W3's, which comes from an idle worker that holds nothing, W10's hand-over, which the controller sends before it parks, W15's, whose holder waits on nothing but a file call, W6's, from the controller to a permit a stage or a start's abort holds, where no permit's holder waits for a permit, W6's, to the reservation a LIST holds, which ends by W2 and W17 and is dropped by the merge (W19), W17's, from a holder of a permit, or a try, to the lookup that holds that permit's token, a blocking-pool task that waits only on the C resolver, and W19's, to the merge, which waits only by W6, W2 and W17. W20's waits end at their 1 s bound whatever the others do. The lifecycle thread waits on nothing in the process: it signals only through atomics and eventfd writes (W9), and its `exit_now` at `T_hard` ends every other wait but a stuck file call (A11), a write or read waiting on a file lock another, throttled process holds, a directory sink's final `syncfs` and head `fsync`s, and the journal's syncs (2.11, *What cannot be bounded*). The core blocks nowhere (core 4.1, *the core never waits and never starts a thread*). No engine lock is held across a core call or a network request.

**Tests.** Every engine test runs under `unityrs_testkit::guard`, which fails a hang instead of waiting forever. The scenes include a stalled sink, a stalled download server, a stdout and a stderr that are never read, both on one pipe read slowly (every stdout line written arrives intact, the `exit` line last; stderr lines may be dropped, and are counted; the caller's flags on the description are unchanged after exit), and a stop in each phase; `a_wedged_attempt_cannot_pass_the_deadline` (a test decoder spins, and the process exits 6 by the deadline); `a_stop_during_staging_exits_within_the_grace`; `a_leaked_charge_stops_the_run_with_70_and_no_deadline`. `the_engine_starts_no_unbounded_wait` is a source scan in the core's style (`unityrs_testkit::waits`): it finds every `recv`, `lock`, `join`, `wait`, `park`, `flock`, `poll` and `libc::poll`, `write` to stderr and `block_on` in `unityrs-batch` and checks each against this table, by module, the `block_on` of phases 5 and 6 against W1 and W2 and each `poll` against W1, W8 and W9, skipping code under the `test-hooks` feature, whose holds are the tests' own, and that no lock guard is alive across a call into `unityrs_core` or an `.await`.

---

## 5. The memory argument

### 5.1 The bound

In the charge unit (`heap_cost`, glibc's chunk, core 3.15), at every instant:

```
heap <= I + C_thr + C_env + C_net + C_spec + C_parse + C_hdr + C_fin + C_base
```

**Proof.**
1. Every allocation that grows with an input or an output is charged before it is made. The core charges its own (core 4.1, *one meter per input, charged before every input-sized allocation*). The engine charges its buffers, the attempt's records of the AssetBundle object, the record buffer (2.6), the sort's runs and buffers, the resume merge's page and the transfers' buffers (2.9) under the same rule.
2. During extraction, each charge books with one attempt's budget, whose limits are its slot, or one peek's, whose `M` is within its slot (2.6 step 4). The controller dispatches while the slots in flight sum to at most `I`, and frees a slot only after its budget read zero (2.2); a leaked slot stays reserved.
3. In phases 6 and 7 no attempt runs, and every charge books with the phase budget, of limit `I`.
4. During a stop, the attempts still in flight hold at most their slots, and frozen transfers at most their phase-budget reservations; neither budget grants anything new beyond its limit, and the finisher allocates only `C_fin`.
5. Each budget refuses any reservation that would pass its limit, in one compare-and-swap.
6. Everything else the process allocates is a term of 5.3, but preflight's response bodies: its head read and the `running` write's read-back, each a buffer of 1 MiB + 1 byte (2.9); the bucket proof's body is dropped as it is read (2.9). They are held only in phases 2 and 3, before any attempt runs, so they fit within `I`. Phase 1's parse, before any attempt and before the finisher exists, holds what it leaves (`C_spec`) and, while it runs, `C_parse` past `I + C_fin` (5.3).

Records, candidates, the winners, the archive member lists, the zip central directory and the manifest's rows live on disk (2.8, 2.9), so no term grows with the number of outputs, skips, members or keys in the store.

Phase-specific audits reuse one account with limit `I`. Phase 6 retains its
40 MiB proof; that audit ceiling is not a global phase-7 peak limit. Phase 7
uses the admission envelopes in 2.9, whose simultaneous charged buffers must
fit `I`. Waiting for admission beside active transfers is scheduling, not an
allocation failure. With no active work, a valid envelope that cannot be
admitted is `internal`; allocator refusals on pool threads are audited as
`host_memory`. Frozen phase work and finisher storage still respect 5.1.

The per-drive publication/row assembly envelope is
`2 MiB + heap_cost(16 MiB) = 18,878,464` bytes. One MiB is charged fixed overhead
for transient paths and adapters; less than another MiB covers the simultaneous
charged cursor, parser and row buffers. Publication allocates one fallible, charged 16 MiB buffer per concurrent drive,
reusing it for hashing and transfer across every winner. Row assembly uses a
separate fixed finisher account of `C_fin` (24 MiB), so it can read frozen
outcomes while stopped phase work retains limit-`I` reservations. Each account
separately fits its admitted envelopes; their overlap is already the `I + C_fin` term. This is independent of winner,
member and record counts. The account's historical peak remains the maximum
across phases; publication audits against `I`, rather than B6's 40 MiB ceiling.
Admission checks precede allocation, while actual allocator refusals are audited
on the pool thread. A counted child on native aarch64 measured 17,118,464 heap
bytes and 18,166,912 charged bytes, with zero kept bytes/charges after each of
159 transfer-path, 147 resume-hit, 162 resume-miss, 159 publication-refusal and
130 globally withheld allocation refusals. The sweep includes directory row
publication; each unblocked serial drive allocates its transfer/hash buffer once. These are local phase
measurements, not B12's process-level constants.
B7c's shared dispatcher and all active drives remain within `I`; the dispatcher
is charged once, and drive count is admitted before dispatch. Finisher rows
and heads use the separate `C_fin` account. Counted normal-finisher children
with 1 and 128 outputs each reached 120 allocation sites, all individually
refused with `host_memory`, zero retained heap and zero outstanding charges.
Both sizes peaked at 17,118,400 counted bytes and 18,166,912 charged bytes,
below 24 MiB. No complete row stream is added to the spool: chunks go directly
to the sink, so the disk ledger stays `6S + 2A`, including outcomes.


### 5.2 Every holder of charged bytes

| holder | budget | taken | released |
|---|---|---|---|
| the attempt's copy of its input's bytes | the attempt's `Input` | its start | its end |
| the job handle's runs, parsed entries, compiled trees, kept errors (core 3.6, 3.15) | `Input` | as read | its end |
| the values of X's objects | `Input` | per object | after that object's outputs |
| a block decode's transients: `DecodeBuf`'s old and new room while it grows, LZMA's dictionary and state | whichever meter decodes | during the decode | at its end |
| export buffers, one sheet group at a time | `Input` | per sheet | when its last output is spooled |
| the AssetBundle object's records, the view's name table, the plan table, the sprite-group table, the record buffer | `Input` | the attempt | its end |
| a member's peek bits (2.6 step 4) | `Input` | the member's reach | the reach's end |
| a member's keys and their sorted permutation, for its dropped rows (2.6 step 4) | `Input` | the member's load | the member's reaches' end |
| a peek's open: the staged bytes, its runs and entries, a decode's transients, its peek file's buffer, all through the peek's one meter (2.6 step 4) | the peek's `Input` | its start | its end |
| the keys of a lookup | the meter of the lookup's `from` | during the call | at its return |
| each private open: a `Path` source's bytes, its runs and entries, the runs its reaches of marked entries cache (2.6 step 4) | the attempt's `Dependency` | load, or reopen after eviction | eviction, or the attempt's end |
| the private registry's slots and keys | `Dependency` | registration | the attempt's end |
| a load's staged registrations (CR1, core 3.13): their list, about 240 B a directory row, and each row's charges, a row whose key was new when staged paying a copy and a share even when an earlier row of the same load takes the key | `Dependency` | the load, before its first key is published | its commit (what the commit keeps becomes the registry's), or its failure |
| a read's handles into another bundle | `Dependency` | the read | the read's end |
| the sort's runs and merge buffers | phase | phase 6 | before phase 7 |
| a LIST page (resume, verification: 40 MiB reserved per call and held until the merge drops the page); a transfer's buffer; a hashing pass (an archive's, a directory destination's) | phase | phase 7 | its end, or `_exit` for a transfer frozen by a stop |
| staged bundles and their peek files, outputs, records, sorted runs, member lists, the outcomes files, row files | disk (spool) | their write | the spool's next start (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 6.2) |
| an archive's central directory | disk (spool) | its transfer, or resume's hashing pass | when that ends (2.9) |

### 5.3 The constant terms

The constants compiled in (`c_base`, `m_thread`, `m_pool`, `m_dispatch`, `m_end`, `c_env`) are **provisional** estimates until `extract.md` X12 measures them, and the bound built from them with them (`BATCH_SPEC.md` section 10's `memory_bound`). The calibration is certified for 4 KiB pages only (`heap_cost` rounds a mapped chunk to a 4 KiB page): on another page size `--check` and a run label the bound uncertified on stderr and go on (`BATCH_SPEC.md` section 10).

B9a resource inventory, within the existing floors (no term raised):

| resource | lifetime inventory and allowance |
|---|---|
| descriptors | self-pipe 2; BOOTTIME timerfd 1; controller/finisher/end wakes 6 (each current `Wake` owns a duplicate writer fd); two Tokio reactors at 2 each on Linux; total 13, within the fixed `RLIMIT_NOFILE` allowance of 72. Readiness registrations add no fd. The 514 reset entries add no reserved or duplicated socket fd. Connections and resolver files stay within `16 × download_concurrency`, workers within `4 × threads` |
| threads | main, controller, lifecycle, progress, finisher plus `threads` workers and `2 × (download_concurrency + 1)` pool threads: `threads + 7 + 2 × download_concurrency`, the existing host floor. Under `stop_on_stdin_eof` the stdin watch is one thread more (the round-1 ruling moved its read off the lifecycle thread); B9b adds it to the `RLIMIT_NPROC` floor when it activates the field. The early writer precedes these spawns. Both new pools use explicit 2 MiB stacks and one shared one-second startup count |
| fixed memory | 514 atomic socket entries (4,112 bytes plus flags/counters), stop/timing atomics, the stdin watch's 4 KiB read and `PIPE_BUF` line buffers, and startup registration/count bookkeeping belong to `C_base`; each const-TLS panic slot fits the existing 2 KiB allowance. Secret references belong to measured `C_spec`/`C_hdr`. No table grows per request or per output |
| runtime/finisher | controller runtime bookkeeping remains `C_base`; the finisher runtime and working set remain `C_fin = 24 MiB`. Prewarm tasks and channels are prepared before mutation. B12 still measures the complete integrated constants; B9a does not claim a final squeeze measurement |

The testkit's scene processes, FIFO readers and pipe drains are test-only and
outside the engine's resource inventory. A scene owns fresh scratch, continuous
FIFO drainage, deliberate kills and cleanup of its child groups before reaping.
Nested scene children inherit the supervisor's group so an interrupted wrapper
cannot strand a separate engine group. Parent-death tests use an isolated
subreaper supervisor; they do not change the test runner's reaping policy.

B7c's normal-run preflight also provisionally uses `--check`'s 6 MiB
environment-block allowance. B12 must measure the inherited block for normal
runs; until then this conservative allowance can refuse a tight host with 69.

| term | value | source |
|---|---|---|
| `C_thr` | `threads x (2 x stack_needed + CONTAINER_LEVEL_STACK x (max_container_depth + 1) + 0.11 MiB + 2 KiB) + 5 x 2 MiB + the environment block + 2 x (download_concurrency + 1) x 2 MiB` | each worker's stack of twice `Limits::stack_needed` (release: 1.75 MiB, `limits/mod.rs`) and the per-level term (release: 3 KiB a level, 17 levels by default; 2.4), counted whole, plus its detection scratch, inflater state and window and tally (0.11 MiB, core 3.15) and its 2 KiB panic slot (2.11); the main, controller, lifecycle, progress and finisher threads at 2 MiB each, the main thread's plus the environment block, and the two runtimes' blocking pools, each `download_concurrency + 1` threads of 2 MiB: set explicitly, the main thread's counted (2.11) |
| `C_env` | `threads x (c_env + max_view_bundles x 1 KiB)` | each private Environment's fixed parts, uncharged (core 3.15, one per Environment), and per registered bundle the uncharged `Meter::OWN_HEAP`, `Dependency` record and two copies of its staged path (`limits/meter.rs`, `env/load.rs`, `env/mod.rs`), 1 KiB at most with `spool.dir` at most 256 bytes (A1); and the attempt's walk of its view, at most `max_view_bundles + 1` entries (2.3). A peek (2.6 step 4) opens one bundle with no Environment, within its worker's share |
| `C_net` | `(2 x download_concurrency + 1) x c_net + download_concurrency x 2 MiB + (2 x download_concurrency + 1) x 64 KiB` | per open connection, its connection and TLS state, `c_net <= 4 MiB` (A1), its 64 KiB streaming buffer included. A connection is one request's future's, and ends with it (2.5), so one is open only for a request in flight: at most the controller's `download_concurrency` permits (stages or transfers) and one LIST, and the finisher's `download_concurrency` requests, so at most `2 x download_concurrency + 1`, a stop's frozen requests among them, and at most `download_concurrency` of them transfers. Plus, per transfer, a multipart upload's part list, reserved at its part count, at most 10,000 parts, 2 MiB with each `ETag` at most 100 bytes (2.9), and per request in flight a response body, at most the connector's 64 KiB (2.9). A lookup's host copies, while it is outstanding, beside no connection of its permit, and its address list, until a `connect` succeeds (2.5), take its permit's `c_net` share; the finisher's spare permit has none, but in step 3 its stale lookups sit beside one connection, within the finisher's `download_concurrency` shares (2.11). Staging streams through the connection's buffer (2.5); upload payloads are in the phase budget |
| `C_spec` | all retained phase-1 heap, measured | the parsed spec and every derived engine structure, measured by the counting allocator's live-bytes count on the main thread across parsing and validation, from step 2's start, as the heap left once the raw bytes are dropped (2.7, 2.11 step 2), so step 1's test-only tables (CR8's, 2.6 step 4) fall outside it, as they fall outside `p`; the controller's per-bundle state and the id permutation are measured too: B12's audit (`tests/memory_constants.rs`) finds every structure the controller keeps per bundle built by `phase1::prepare` inside the measure, and what it holds after its last commit equal to what phase 1 kept at 8, 32 and 128 bundles, so no per-bundle allowance is added (the former 256 B). Preflight computes the bound after validation, so it uses the measured figure, and no parse factor is assumed (A1) |
| `C_parse` | `max(0, p - C_spec - I - C_fin)` | `p`, phase 1's peak live bytes on the main thread above their count at step 2's start (the main thread resets its peak to its live count at step 2's start; `p` is that peak less that count, 2.7), which 2.7's count records from `JOB.json`'s read to validation's end: the raw bytes, the parser's scratch and its structures' growth among them, measured. Phase 1 runs before any attempt, any phase-budget charge and the finisher, so of `p` above what it leaves (`C_spec`) only what passes `I + C_fin` adds |
| `C_hdr` | `heap_cost`, summed, of the distinct header values, the name-to-value table, the masker's entries, `4 x download_concurrency` copies of the largest bundle's header block, and, with an object store sink, 6 of the credentials at their caps (16.25 KiB; a directory sink reads none) | each `{"env": NAME}` header variable is read once into one shared `Bytes`-backed value, at most 16 KiB (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 1.2), however many bundles name it, the masker sharing it (2.11 step 2), and step 6's table maps each variable's name to it; each stage in flight serializes its bundle's headers, names and resolved values, into its request's `HeaderMap` and hyper's encoded head, whose growth peaks at 3 copies (2.7's `realloc` rule); and an object store sink's credentials, at their caps (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 1.6), once for each of their six holders: the engine's read, the controller's three store clients, the finisher's, and the masker. The values, the table, the masker's entries and the blocks are known at the main thread's step 6 (2.11), before the bound is checked |
| `C_fin` | 24 MiB | the finisher's 16 MiB chunk buffer, its 1 MiB read buffer and its outcomes buffer, the final write's head read-back (a buffer of 1 MiB + 1 byte, 2.9, `BATCH_SPEC.md` 7.2, parsed into a typed struct that ignores other fields), its journal read buffer and the ids of its aborts in flight, at most `download_concurrency` (2.11's step 1), its runtime and its panic and progress slots (2.11) |
| `C_base` | `c_base + threads x m_thread + 2 x (download_concurrency + 1) x m_pool + download_concurrency x 2,080 B + threads x m_dispatch + threads x m_end + 16 KiB + archives x 160 B` | `c_base`, `m_thread` and `m_pool` are constants compiled into the binary, which X12 measures (`extract.md`): `c_base` as the most, over the run's samples, of the heap less only what the sample measures (the budgets' held charges, never a reservation's unused part, the finisher thread's own count, `C_spec` and `C_parse`, and the header and credential bytes the scene holds, at their live sizes) and the `m_*` terms at the scene's sizes, no term at its cap, on a scene with a failed input, an archive, an old journal, a resume run, verification's LIST and a lookup held outstanding through the `resolve` hook's delay, at `threads` and `download_concurrency` 1, run with each sink and the larger pinned; `m_thread` and `m_pool` are the heap one more worker, and one more blocking-pool thread of each runtime, adds, a lookup's task cell among it: each pool has a thread for every task it can have outstanding (2.11), so the threads bound the cells, and B needs no new term, as it needs none for the static socket table (2.11). It holds the binary, both runtimes, the rustls `ClientConfig` (2.11 step 4), the progress thread's slots, the controller's 64 KiB zero buffer (2.9) and its journal read buffer at the spool's start (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 6.2 step 4). The rest grows with the spec: the start's abort ids in flight, a key and an upload id each (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 2.3, 2.9); the controller's queues (2.4) at their capacities, each message at the largest size, its heap included, of a dispatch (`m_dispatch`) or an end message (`m_end`, one end-channel slot's size: the actual end union, its bounded repaired entry detail included, plus channel-slot overhead, so `threads x m_end` covers std's array slots, X12 measuring it with the channel at capacity, 2.4), compiled in too; a journal removal's one held line, at most 16 KiB (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 6.1); and resume's merge's kept `ETag` (at most 100 bytes, 2.9) and size per spec archive, 160 B with its entry |

There is no provider term: version 1 configures no typetree provider (A8), so the provider cache (`ProviderChain::DEFAULT_CACHE_BYTES`, 64 MiB, one per `ProviderChain` shared by every cloned context; core 3.15, as CR5 corrected it) holds nothing.

Startup's fixed scratch belongs to `C_base`: one reusable head-read buffer of
1 MiB + 1 byte, one 16 KiB host-file line buffer beside a 1 KiB read buffer,
and transient fallibly allocated paths. Host inventories stream through that
line buffer; a line exceeding it is refused as the corresponding unreadable
procfs/cgroup file. Rooted destination components and temp names use fixed
256-byte storage, with no allocation per output inventory. These are separate
from phase 7's buffer charges and the finisher's `C_fin`; B12 still measures
`C_base` and the other provisional constants.

The transport reserves a conservative 3.5 MiB envelope per active connection for the third-party state and full backing capacities, plus `heap_cost` of each fallibly allocated copied DATA piece (at most 64 KiB). A slice's length never substitutes for its backing allocation. Moved buffers keep their charge and permit until released. A lookup, local copy or moved write owns the permit's sole blocking-operation token until its closure returns, even after timeout; the existing pool capacity bounds all outstanding operations. The shared TLS configuration and runtime bookkeeping stay in `C_base`, header references in measured phase 1 and values in `C_hdr`.

A lookup separately retains a 512 KiB Rust-side reservation in its blocking closure, including its fallible host copy and fixed first-sixteen address output, so timeout releases neither its storage charge nor its operation token early. While it is live, the service reserves 3 MiB; after it returns, the service grows that reservation to 3.5 MiB before connecting and TLS. Lookup and connection storage therefore reserve at most 3.5 MiB together. NSS C allocations remain in 5.4's separate allowance.

Source checks on hyper 1.12.0 confirm the complete-head `>` and trailer `>=` thresholds (`proto/h1/role.rs`, `proto/h1/decode.rs`); rustls 0.23.46 bounds a deframed handshake message at 65,535 bytes (`msgs/deframer/handshake.rs`), with compression disabled here. Per-item certificate/authority allocations still need the counted TLS 1.2 stress scene.

Engine-owned byte buffers use fallible reservation. The approved libraries still allocate infallibly: notably http 1.5.0's `HeaderMap::try_reserve` checks its entry ceiling, not allocator refusal. Fixed startup `Arc` allocations are infallible too. These are not claimed as recoverable allocation sites; their prepaid envelopes and counted acceptance gates remain essential.

The reservation is not a measurement of hyper or rustls. Source revalidation on the exact locked graph and `http_alloc`'s counted maximum-head, maximum-trailer, backing-overlap and loopback TLS scenes are acceptance gates against the unchanged 4 MiB allowance. The test excludes the peer, includes blocking-pool allocations, and counts realloc growth overlaps. An excess requires a bound ruling before URL enablement; B12 owns full calibration.

### 5.4 Outside the charge unit

- **glibc's slack.** A live allocation whose chunk is below 128 KiB may hold up to 16 bytes past its charge (`CHUNK_SLACK`), at most half again of those allocations' charges (core 4.1, *one meter per input*; `limits/mod.rs`). So in glibc's own bytes the worst case is 1.5 times 5.1's bound, the figure 2.7's resource-limit check uses. The budgets are dominated by large mapped buffers (runs, pixels, PNG output), whose pages cover the slack, so the excess is a few percent in practice. The nightly reports it.
- **Allocator retention.** glibc raises its mmap threshold each time a mapped chunk is freed, after which large buffers come from arenas that do not return memory (`limits/mod.rs`; `tests/heap_rounding.rs`). `extract.md` E57: the binary calls `mallopt(M_MMAP_THRESHOLD, 128 KiB)` and sets `M_ARENA_MAX` to `threads + 2` at start. Fragmentation of small chunks inside arenas is not bounded by the core's rules (A7). The nightly measures peak RSS against the bound.
- **`getaddrinfo`'s heap.** Each running lookup, at most `2 x (download_concurrency + 1)`, allocates its answers through glibc's `malloc`, unseen by the counting allocator: an A and an AAAA answer, each retried over TCP up to 64 KiB when truncated, and an entry for each address they hold, about 6,400 at most: an estimated 1.5 MiB a lookup at worst on glibc's `files dns` path (another NSS module may cost more, `BATCH_SPEC.md` section 6), so `2 x (download_concurrency + 1) x 1.5 MiB` in all (27 MiB at `download_concurrency` 8, 0.75 GiB at 256), and a few KiB for an ordinary answer. 5.6's cgroup rule and 2.7's `RLIMIT_AS` and `RLIMIT_DATA` floor each add it, as `3 MiB x (download_concurrency + 1)`. The nightly's `VmHWM` against the bound checks it.
- **A spool in memory.** On tmpfs or ramfs the spool's bytes are memory that a memory cgroup counts and only swap can reclaim; preflight refuses such a spool (`spool_in_memory`, `BATCH_SPEC.md` 6.2).
- **Page cache.** Spool writes and file reads fill the kernel's page cache: reclaimable and outside the heap, but counted by a memory cgroup (A7). The spool writer drops the pages of outputs, sorted runs and row files after closing them (`posix_fadvise(DONTNEED)`), and keeps staged bundles cached, since attempts read them again.

### 5.5 Arithmetic on the corpus

**A decode's transient** (`limits/vec.rs`, `budget/mod.rs`, `codec/lzma.rs`, `files/bundle/ahead.rs`).
- `DecodeBuf` doubles its room up to the declared end of what it decodes: a block's declared size, or a run's blocks', which its reader reads ahead as far as the room can double. Past that end, where an LZMA block's output may run (core 3.6), it doubles up to its cap. While it grows, the old room is charged beside the new (`grow_counted`). `into_bytes` gives the spare room back in place and charges nothing beside it, moving only a mapped block given back below 128 KiB (`shrink_charged`).
- So a decode peaks at its last growth, `c_(n-1) + c_n`, below `3N` for `N` the most room it asked (its declared end, or past it its output and one LZMA window of at most 1 MiB). An output that ends at its declared end, as a well-formed block's or run's does, ends its room there: `c_n` is its `B` bytes, and the peak is below `2B`.
- LZMA adds its dictionary, `min(clamp(declared, 4 KiB, 8 MiB), named)` plus 576 B, and 33,040 B of state.

For the corpus's largest input, 8.1 MB holding one LZMA block declared and decoded at 69.4 MB, the last growth is from 64 MiB (67.1 MB) to 69.4 MB, both charged at once. With the input's bytes and the decoder's 0.56 MB that is 145.2 MB, the peak of the input's meter that core 3.15 measured (`tools/perf/perf.py`'s serial pass, which keeps no value): the measured peak is that growth.

**One attempt's Input peak** is `P_X = s_X + runs_X + transient_X + meta_X + values_X + export_X`:
- `s_X`, X's file size; `runs_X`, at most the declared decoded size plus one more copy of each block a span straddles (core 3.6); `transient_X`, what the largest decode holds beside its output: its last growth's old room, below `B_X` for a well-formed run of `B_X` bytes, and LZMA's dictionary and 33 KiB of state, the dictionary 8 MiB at most unless a retry after a data error widened it to `min(named, 2 x produced)` (`codec/lzma.rs`, `decode`); `values_X`, the objects being exported, one at a time, each holding its own charge and either a copy of the bytes its slices name or, below `pin::worth_copying`'s threshold (`pin.rs`), slices of a run `runs_X` holds (core 3.15, *What a kept value holds*);
- `export_X` for one sheet group of `L` x `H` pixels is at most `max(16/3 LH, S_src) + 4 LH + 4.53 LH + 1.2 H + 5 (4 L + 1) + 407,600 + 33 KiB` for a plain texture, its terms: the texture's source data, `S_src` its length as stored (`16/3 LH` is `4 LH x 4/3`, four bytes a pixel with every mip; a thin texture's mip chain can store more, so the larger counts); the image being encoded, the sheet (`4 LH`); the PNG output, one allocation at `deflateBound` of its `4 LH + H` filtered bytes (2.6 step 10), which at memLevel 9 is `1.1309 x (4 LH + H)` plus 10 B, with 12 B a chunk and 46 B of framing, within `4.53 LH + 1.2 H` for a texture of about 10,300 pixels or more and within it plus the slack below for a smaller one, whose 10 B, one chunk and framing (68 B) it does not cover; the filter rows, `5 (4 L + 1)`; and deflate's state, 407,600 B, the sum of its five allocations' `heap_cost` (zlib 1.2.13 at E19's windowBits 15 and memLevel 9: a 64 KiB window, `prev` 64 KiB, `head` and the pending buffer 128 KiB each, which `zalloc`'s 8-byte header puts past the 128 KiB mmap threshold at 132 KiB each, and the state). The 33 KiB is the plain texture form's counted slack: `heap_cost` charges a buffer rounded up to 16 B past an 8 B header with a 32 B minimum, or, when mapped, to a 4 KiB page past that, at most 4,119 B over its request; at most eight of the buffers are mapped (the source, the image, the output and the five rows), 32,952 B, deflate's state being counted exactly, and the PNG output's 68 B for a small texture come to 33,020 B. By the crates' rules, RGBA32 with every mip, the most a texture's slack is used, needs 31,783 B of it at 32,768 x 1, 30,800 B at 65,536 x 1 and 28,989 B at 32,768 x 8, and no size of a sweep, every power-of-two size up to 2^28 pixels and about 30,000 others, with no mip or every mip, needs more than the first. Sprites and atlases add their alpha texture's source data, `max(16/3 LH, its S_src)`, and the RGBA and alpha sheets, `4 LH` each, three more mappable buffers, so their slack is about 12.4 KB more (3 x 4,119 B), about 45 KB in all; that form is an estimate until the sprite and atlas paths are measured (section 8). So `export_X` is about `13.9 LH` for a plain texture and `27.2 LH` for sprites and atlases, plus about 0.4 MiB: 2048 x 2048 takes about 56 MiB plain and 109 MiB for sprites, 4096 x 4096 about 435 MiB for sprites. Measured on both builds (section 8), the plain form bounds every texture, at 1.035 times the peak at the least, and the crates' rules past them, thin textures included; the earlier form, `16/3 LH + 4 LH + 4.52 LH` with the rows and the state, was below the measured peak for 640 textures a build, all of at most 4,096 pixels, by at most 281 B, and, by the crates' rules, for RGBA32 with every mip at every size (by 29.5 KB at 2048 x 2048), since full mips use all of the source term's slack. The exact figure needs no form: `unityrs_texture::plan()` and `unityrs_imageio::plan()` give every charge before anything is allocated. A sprite's mesh arrays add a few bytes per vertex and index.

**One attempt's Dependency peak** is the private registry (about 300 B per CAB of the view, A4), and, while a member loads, its staged registrations, the list and every row's charges until the load's commit (core 3.13, CR1): about 240 B a row for the list, a row whose key an earlier row of that load took paying a copy and a share, and a row whose key is already registered holding its `lowered` name beside the slot it replaces, which stays charged until then; plus the opens its reads keep within `D`, the blocks its reaches of marked entries cache (X's own included: its slot decodes them on `D`, 2.6 step 4), and one decode's transient. On the corpus the largest view's dependencies decode to 87.5 to 89.4 MB with views by `m_Dependencies`, and to 116.0 to 128.4 MB with views by the builds' manifest dependency lists, so at `D` = 256 MiB no attempt evicts for its reads (A5). Those are the figures every attempt's peek decoded in `D` before the table; with it, only the reaches of marked entries decode there, and, until CR7, the core's per-attempt peeks (2.6 step 4). Under CR9 (section 6), an opted-in member's cut decodes and the live runs and slices of its block's earlier decodes add a term: CR9's own PR states the exact bound; every byte is charged, so 5.1 holds.

### 5.6 Sizing rules, and an example

Each rule is a recommendation the manifest reports against, not a validation.

| goal | rule |
|---|---|
| every input that fits at all completes | `M >= max(input_peak, dependency_peak, ceil(decode_work_peak / 4), the largest staged size)` from an earlier run's manifest (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 7.3; `max_decode_work` is `4 M`, 2.2), with `peek_peak` and `ceil(peek_work_peak / 4)` in that max, the peeks' figures (2.4), a guide, since `decode_work_peak` misses some work (7.3), and above the configured value for every input or bundle that failed on an `M` reason, a bundle's peek failure read from its `bundles` row's `peek` (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 7.3), which shows one that no record names, when a member of lesser id failed first; a bundle's peek runs within `M` and its own `4 M`, and one that fails fails its holders with its own reason, an `M` reason (a `Limit` of its meter), its open's error or another data limit (2.6 step 4); X13's need (`extract.md`) is the exact figure; and `D >= max` dependency peak, the blocks registration's reaches of marked entries decode included, and, until CR7, the blocks the interim's per-attempt peeks decode (2.6 step 4, 5.5) |
| full parallelism | `I >= threads x (M + D)` |
| no input fails on export work | `max_export_work >= export_work_peak` from an earlier run's manifest (`BATCH_SPEC.md` 7.3), and above the configured value for every input that failed on it |
| the spool fits | `spool.size_bytes >= the staged bundles, each at its claimed size, + their peek files + S + threads x W + 6 x S + 2 x A + the download_concurrency largest central directories`, where the peek files (2.6 step 4) are one per bundle with a nested container, a block of `b` per `8 x b` of its top-level rows, `W = max_export_work x k_blk` bounds one input's claims, `S = spooled_parts x k_blk` (2.6 step 10) bounds the inputs' claims and their records' counted size, one input's at most `W`, `A = sum(round_up(e(key) + 512, b))` over the spec's declared archives, where `e(key)` is the exact JSON-escaped byte length of the key as the archive row writer emits it, excluding quotes, bounds one maximal archive row per archive, and `6 x S + 2 x A` holds phase 6's files and phase 7's outcomes by 2.9's ledger, the archive row stream and its private metadata each within `A`, its extraction-derived row files among them: one sort's runs and its merged output, beside the step outputs later steps and the finisher read, each line at most twice its record's counted size, runs and intermediate outputs deleted once read (2.9). `S` from an earlier run's `spooled_parts`, plus `W` for each input whose output is not known, keeps one hostile input from stopping the run with `spool_full`: the claim refused there is a run failure (2.7), since what the spool holds depends on the order inputs ran in |
| the spool's filesystem holds what no claim counts | free space at start `>= size_bytes + reserve_bytes +` the directories (about `inputs x 2 x st_blksize`, and an entry per spooled file) `+` the inodes' space where the filesystem allocates it (XFS: about 512 B each) `+` the uploads journal (a line per upload in flight or whose abort failed, at most `download_concurrency` plus the failed aborts, each its endpoint, bucket, key and an upload id under 1 KiB, 2.9; twice that while a removal rewrites it, `BATCH_SPEC.md` 6.1) `+` the sink's written bytes when the output root shares the spool's filesystem; and free inodes `>=` the spooled files `+ 2 x inputs`, the spooled files being at most `spooled_parts / 4 KiB`, plus `W / 4 KiB` for each input whose output is not known, plus the staged bundles and their peek files, phase 6's files and the row files; `reserve_bytes` is the filesystem's own margin (5.7) |
| `I` above about 7 GiB | `vm.max_map_count` above its stock 65,530, which 2.7's `map_count_too_low` needs once `I` passes `(65,530 - 8,192) x 128 KiB`, about 7 GiB; under Kubernetes it is the node's sysctl, not the pod's |
| a cgroup v2 memory limit holds the run | `BATCH_SPEC.md` section 6's limit: 1.5 times the bound, for glibc's slack (5.4); 10 MiB for each of `C_net`'s `2 x download_concurrency + 1` live sockets, whose buffers a v2 cgroup charges, at `tcp_rmem`'s and `tcp_wmem`'s maxima (6 MiB and 4 MiB by Linux's defaults); 1.5 MiB for each of the `2 x (download_concurrency + 1)` lookups' `getaddrinfo` heaps, at 5.4's estimate; and the spool's dirty pages, which the kernel holds to `vm.dirty_ratio` percent of the cgroup's memory, hence the divisor, or to `vm.dirty_bytes`, hence the sum, reclaiming the clean ones under the limit (A7). No arena term: an arena's 64 MiB is address space, which only `RLIMIT_AS` counts (2.7) |
| a directory sink's final `syncfs` stays short | the spool on another filesystem than the output root, so its writes are not that `syncfs`'s (2.11). That does not shorten dirty-page throttling, whose limits are the host's global one and the memory cgroup's, each across filesystems (A11) |

**Example for the corpus:** `threads` 8, `download_concurrency` 8, `M` = `D` = 256 MiB, `I` = 4 GiB.
- The constants come to about 235 MiB: 75 MiB of thread stacks (39 MiB for workers and the five named threads, the environment block aside, 36 MiB for the two blocking pools), 9 MiB of private Environments at `max_view_bundles` 1,024, 85 MiB for `C_net`'s 17 connections, 8 part lists and 17 response bodies, about 21 MiB of spec and controller state for a full build's 12,000 bundles (a spec of about 4.8 MB), 24 MiB for the finisher, about 20 MiB of base, and `C_hdr`, about 1 MiB with header blocks under 32 KiB; `C_parse` is 0, the parse's peak being far below `I`.
- So the bound is about 4.23 GiB in the charge unit, about 6.34 GiB (1.5 times it) under glibc's worst-case slack, and a memory cgroup about 8.2 GiB at Linux's default `vm.dirty_ratio` of 20.
- The corpus's largest input peaks at 145.2 MB, in its decode (5.5), inside `M`. Its texture export holds 124.6 MiB at its largest texture's, its own 74 MiB beside the corpus's largest export peak of 50.5 MiB, and no other input passes 54 MiB (section 8). So the corpus needs an `M` of about 140 MiB, and 256 MiB stays the example: about 1.8 times that, with room for the sprite path's sheet groups, which 5.5's estimate puts near 183 MiB beside that input's 74 MiB. Every slot reserves 512 MiB, so the 8 slots reserve 4 GiB.
- The spool, at `max_export_work`'s default of 2 x `M` (512 MiB, so `threads x W` = 4 GiB at `k_blk` 1), with no archive and no nested container, so no peek file: the staged bundles (1.79 GB claimed on build A, 1.34 GB on build B, 0.92 GB on the preview diff) `+ S + 4 GiB + 6 x S`, with `S` about 1.91 GB a full build and 0.35 GB on the diff (A5), asks about 18 GiB a full build and 7 GiB on the diff. The final default is `max_export_work = 2 × M`. Full export-path measurements
including records and member work found the largest export work at about
139.8 MiB on build A (a texture input), just under it on build B (about
137.3 MiB), and p99 about 10.6 MiB. At the example `M = 256 MiB`, 512 MiB is
about 3.7 times the measured maximum; at the measured minimum `M` near
140 MiB it is about twice the maximum. Proportional sizing also accommodates
larger-`M` runs. This default is final; it is a bounded-work policy and explicit
overrides remain supported. B12's memory-bound constants remain provisional.

Archives add only the largest-active-central-directories term from 2.9 to spool claims. Complete ZIP bytes go directly to sink temps. Sink provisioning must cover existing outputs plus replacement temps while they coexist, including when spool and sink share a filesystem; those bytes remain outside `spool.size_bytes`.

The spool sizing rule also uses `A = sum(round_up(e(archive_key) + 512, b))`,
where `b` is the preflight block size and `e` is the actual archive-row encoder's
escaped byte length, quotes excluded. Accepted phase-6 verification under
this encoded-key rule is a prerequisite of B7b. B7 extends that ledger through
the outcomes files and finishing, with no second complete overlay stream:
finisher chunks go directly to the sink. Claims exclude filesystem metadata,
inodes and sink temps/outputs; the separate filesystem provisioning rule
above includes them, particularly when spool and sink share a filesystem.

### 5.7 Disk

`spool.size_bytes` bounds the sum of the staged bundles and their peek files, every committed candidate and its records until phase 7, the partial outputs of the attempts in flight, phase 6's sorted runs and member lists, archives' central directories while they stream, the outcomes files, and the manifest's row files. A free-space check against `spool.reserve_bytes` runs before each claim. A refused claim stops the run (2.7). The directories' own blocks (`attempts/` and `done/`, one or more per input) and the files' inodes are claimed by nothing: 5.6 provisions them. One input's claims total at most `max_export_work x k_blk` (2.6 step 10).

### 5.8 How it is checked

- `every_attempt_returns_its_charges` (3.1), and `the_slots_in_flight_never_exceed_the_inflight_bound`, the peek pass's included (2.6 step 4).
- X12 in `extract.md`'s gate: the core's counting allocator (`unityrs_testkit`) over the engine's export paths. The heap never holds more than the charges plus 5.3's constants, as `tests/memory_model.rs` checks for the core.
- X13 in `extract.md`'s gate: each limit squeezed in its own pass, below and at each testkit input's need, each input also as a variant whose outputs expand into archive members (`tests/limit_squeeze.rs`: each need bisected and checked monotone at its adjacent and sampled values, each clear sample the baseline's result, the need and one above and below it each run twice in fresh children, the run below `M`'s need equal, row for row, to the records a reference model derives from the baseline and its skips by `BATCH_SPEC.md` 4.4, 4.5, 4.7 and 7.3, its skips' phases and its export work read from the engine's `test-hooks` tap (`unityrs_batch::work::tap`), every bundle's peek at its own `M` need, and CR7's exclusions held to the catalogue's structure by the core's registry key). The component probes of 5.3's terms that exist today (`m_end` with the end channel full, `c_env`, the per-bundle audit) are `tests/memory_constants.rs`, on the testkit's ledger, which counts a block from before `System` is asked for it until after it is freed, so it never holds less than the live heap.
- `a_stop_in_each_phase_stays_within_the_bound`: the counting allocator over the finisher's streaming.
- `rss_tracks_live_heap_after_large_frees`, with and without E57's settings.
- The nightly writes `VmHWM` into the manifest and checks it against 5.1's bound for its spec.

---

## 6. Requests to the core

Each request goes through the core's integration rule. None changes a core 4.1 property but core 4.1's "decodes no data block", which has two exceptions: a dropped row's first bytes, read today at the load that drops it, and CR7's reaches of the rows its hint marks, with its peeks below depth 1; and CR9, a per-load opt-in, which widens core 3.13's differences (c) and (d) and changes core section 0's decision 4, core 3.6's lazy-read, **Runs**, **Decode work** and **What a read costs** bullets, the module docs' **Block outcomes**, `BundleCounts` and 4.1's bounded-work row (below). And `EnvOptions::unitypy()` is untouched by all of them.

| request | change | property it adds, and its test | what the engine does until it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CR1, landed** | `load_dependency` publishes nothing when it fails. Under `Lazy`, `batch()`'s mode, the load stages its registrations, each charged, then charges its LRU entry (and a listed `find_file` load's `files` entry), and publishes them only once every charge is made, so a registration refused at any entry has inserted no key and there is nothing to remove (`env/load.rs`, `try_load_dep` and `register_staged`; `env/registry/register.rs`, `Stage`, `prepare`, `apply` and `commit`; core 3.13). Nothing after the first key is published fails the load: the commit's `settle` keeps a refusal to itself and leaves the slot listed for a later call. A resolver's load made as a dependency is staged the same way; a caller's load, and any load under `Eager`, `unitypy()`'s mode, registers key by key and keeps, when it fails, what it registered (UnityPy's partial registration), so `unitypy()` is untouched. The stage's transient moves where a load near `D` is refused (5.2, A4) | a failed load leaves the registry's keys as they were and returns every charge (core 4.1, *a failed dependency load publishes nothing*). Tests: `tests/env/failed.rs` `a_failed_load_leaves_every_lookup_as_it_was`, a bundle whose load is refused at each of its dependency reservations in turn, so at its k-th entry for every k, after another bundle registered two same-named CABs, by `load_dependency` and through the resolver; every lookup after it answers as before the load and the pool holds what it held; and the same file's sweeps of a single refusal, of the meter's bound, of another thread's lookups during the load, and of a refusal in the commit's `settle` | landed, so no interim applies. The attempt still fails X on any failed load of its view (2.3, rule 1) and makes no read through that Environment after it (2.6 step 4), now a defence only. Granted, an attempt could skip a failed member that its reads never reach; D does not need it |
| **CR2, wanted** | `Error::refusal(&self) -> Option<Refusal>`, with the same on `DerefError` and `ParseError`: `Refusal::{Budget(Reservation), Limit(LimitKind, MeterKind), Allocator(Reservation), Io}`, where `MeterKind` says whether the refused meter was a dependency's. a nested `span_reads` or `reserve_span_reads` panics; `f` must not wait on a thread that reads); with no table reserved, the core records and allocates nothing (a nested bundle's node is allocated at its open only while a table is reserved, and a bundle opened while none is reserved is never counted, nor one nested in it, opened after a reserve or not, so a test reserves before its load; a dependency loaded before a reserve and reopened after it is counted under its load's id, which the names never list, as its draw found no table); a reserve frees the tables it replaces once their lock is let go, so an allocator's code run by that free may read them; Test: each kind counted once per refusal, absorbed or returned, and each span read once, a cached `entry()` hit not at all; a read under the `bare` key; a load id kept across a `with_room` retry; a nested bundle inheriting its load's id; bundles opened before a reserve untracked after it, nested ones included, and a reopen after it counted under an unnamed id; a reserve's old tables freed with the lock let go; a TPK's total refusal counted at its walk's first ask, once, and the NGUI register's input-bytes refusal once (their crates' `test-counters` features, in CI's feature step); the load-name table's mapping; overflow by a longer name and by a deeper path | landed, so no interim applies: X13, X10's attempt-level `refuse` cases and X11's count of registration's reads can now be built on it.
